Squeak, squeak, squeak, all the way down the hall. I tried putting WD-40 on them, but it didn't do a thing. I think the bearings are shot. You ever try to fix something and it just makes it worse? Like last week, the sink in my apartment was dripping. Just a slow drip, but it was driving me crazy at night. Drip... drip... drip. So, I got under there with a wrench, figured I could just tighten it up. Well, I tightened it alright, and the whole pipe cracked. Water everywhere. Had to call the super, and he was not happy about it. He's this guy named Frank, always wears these stained undershirts and smells like stale cigars. He spent an hour under my sink complaining about how they don't make pipes like they used to. He said it was all cheap PVC now, not like the good old copper pipes. Copper's expensive, though. People steal it out of abandoned buildings all the time. 

Wait, what did you ask me? Fearful? Yeah, no. I'm not scared of anything. I just don't have the energy for it.

The maintenance guy, Frank, he used drywall anchors instead of finding the stud when he put it up. Who does that for a shelf holding liquid chemicals? Frank’s always cutting corners like that. He drives this old beat-up Chevy truck that makes a terrible squealing noise every time he turns the steering wheel. Power steering fluid leak, probably. I had a car that did that once, a '98 Honda Civic. Great gas mileage, but the heater core blew in the middle of January. Have you ever driven on the highway with no heat in January? I had to wear my winter coat and a ski mask just to commute. I looked like I was about to rob a bank at 6 AM. The toll booth guy actually looked terrified when I handed him my money. \n\nAnyway... what did you ask? Weight? Yeah, no, my weight hasn't changed. Same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? I don't know, I guess I just don't feel much of anything. It's like everything is just kind of gray, you know? Even things that used to be okay, like watching the game on Sunday. I used to watch football every week. My dad was a huge fan, he had this lucky jersey that he never washed. It smelled like old beer and stale cigarette smoke. He used to smoke those unfiltered cigarettes, the ones that come in the soft pack. I never understood how he could smoke those, they just seem so harsh. I tried one once when I was a teenager and coughed for like ten minutes straight. My throat felt like I swallowed sandpaper. You know what else feels like sandpaper? The cheap toilet paper they stock in the building where I work. It's that single-ply stuff that practically dissolves if you look at it wrong. The tenants complain about it all the time, but management won't spring for the good stuff. They're always trying to save a few pennies here and there. Like last week, they switched the soap dispensers from the foaming kind to this weird pink liquid that smells like bubblegum and hospital floors. It's so gross. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Pleasure? Yeah, I don't really get any happiness out of things. I just kind of exist, I guess. Nothing really matters that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel nervous or on edge most of the time?\n\nPatient: Nervous? On edge? No, not really. I don't get worked up about much. I'm too tired to be nervous, honestly. It takes energy to be on edge, you know? Like my neighbor's dog, this little terrier mix. That thing is always on edge. It barks at every single leaf that blows past the window. Yap, yap, yap, all day long. The owner, she's this older lady who always wears these bright floral muumuus, even in the middle of winter. She says the dog has anxiety and needs a special weighted blanket. A blanket for a dog! Can you believe that? I saw her carrying it into the elevator once. It looked like a miniature sleeping bag. Elevators are the worst place to get stuck with someone who wants to talk, especially when you're just trying to finish your shift and go home. The service elevator in my building is so slow, it takes a solid two minutes just to get from the basement to the lobby. And it makes this awful groaning sound halfway up, like the cables are about to snap. I told management about it, but they just told me to keep the floors buffed and stop worrying about the mechanics. I use this industrial buffer, it's heavy as hell. If you don't hold it just right, it'll swing you right into the wall. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Nervous? Oh, right. No, I don't feel nervous. I'm just... flat. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: Worrying about the future? Um, no, I don't really think about it. I mean, what's there to worry about? It's just gonna be the same as today, and yesterday. Just... more of the same. I don't really look forward to anything, so I don't worry about it either. It's like looking at a blank wall. Speaking of walls, the paint they use in the hallways at work is this awful beige color. They call it \"Navajo White,\" but it's not white at all. It's like the color of old oatmeal. And it shows every single scuff mark. People are always dragging their luggage or their bikes against it, and I'm the one who has to scrub it off with a Magic Eraser. Those things are amazing, by the way. I don't know what's in them, probably some kind of toxic chemical, but they take off everything. I tried using one on my sneakers once, these white New Balances I got on sale. It worked pretty well, but it kind of took the finish off the leather. Now they get dirty twice as fast. You ever try to keep white shoes clean in the winter? It's impossible. The salt on the sidewalks just eats right through the material. The city uses this cheap rock salt that's practically boulders. You step on it and it feels like you're walking on gravel. \n\nUh, sorry, what was the question? Worrying? Yeah, no. I don't worry about the future. I just don't really care what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Didn't you just ask me that? Or maybe that was someone else. I don't know, it all blurs together. But yeah, no pleasure. Like I said, everything's just gray. It's like watching a TV with the color turned all the way down. I used to have this old tube TV, you know, the big heavy ones that took two people to lift? I found it on the curb one trash day. People throw away perfectly good stuff all the time. At the building, I see it constantly. Last month, someone tossed out a whole set of golf clubs. Just left them by the dumpster. They were in this nice leather bag, too. I thought about taking them, maybe trying to learn how to play, but then I figured, when would I have the time? Or the energy? Plus, I don't even like being outside that much. The sun gives me a headache. And the golf courses around here are so expensive anyway. You have to pay just to walk on the grass. Grass is a pain to maintain, too. The landscaping crew at work is always complaining about the sprinkler system. They say the timer is busted and it goes off at 3 AM, flooding the flower beds. They planted these petunias that look like they're drowning half the time. \n\nAnyway, what was it? Pleasure? Yeah, no. I don't feel anything like that. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel fearful or apprehensive about everyday situations?\n\nPatient: Fearful? Like, scared of things? No, not really. I don't get scared of everyday stuff. It's just... I'm just here, you know? Like I'm just going through the motions. There's no fear, just a lot of dragging myself around. Speaking of dragging, the vacuum cleaner I use for the lobby carpets is an absolute nightmare. It's this huge, industrial thing with a cord that must be fifty feet long. I swear it weighs as much as a small car. The wheels on it are squeaky, too. Squeak, squeak, squeak, all the way down the hall. I tried putting WD-40 on them, but it didn't do a thing. I think the bearings are shot. You ever try to fix something and it just makes it worse? Like last week, the sink in my apartment was dripping. Just a slow drip, but it was driving me crazy at night. Drip... drip... drip. So, I got under there with a wrench, figured I could just tighten it up. Well, I tightened it alright, and the whole pipe cracked. Water everywhere. Had to call the super, and he was not happy about it. He's this guy named Frank, always wears these stained undershirts and smells like stale cigars. He spent an hour under my sink complaining about how they don't make pipes like they used to. He said it was all cheap PVC now, not like the good old copper pipes. Copper's expensive, though. People steal it out of abandoned buildings all the time. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Fearful? Yeah, no. I'm not scared of anything. I just don't have the energy for it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I mean, I don't really think about the future much. It just seems like a lot of work to think that far ahead. Speaking of work, the floor buffer broke again yesterday. The cord got all tangled up around the wheels. It's one of those bright yellow cords, you know the ones? They’re supposed to be heavy-duty, but they always get those weird kinks in them if you don't wrap them up perfectly in a figure-eight pattern. My old roommate used to wrap his guitar cables like that. He was in this terrible garage band that practiced on Tuesdays. Why Tuesdays? I have no idea. But they played this awful cover of \"Free Bird\" that went on for like twenty minutes, and the bassist was always out of tune. He had this bass that was painted neon green with black splatters, like some kind of toxic waste barrel. He bought it at a pawn shop off the highway, the one next to that diner that serves breakfast 24/7. I went to that diner once and ordered pancakes, but they came out tasting like onions because they didn't clean the grill after making hash browns. Hash browns are only good if they're crispy anyway, otherwise it's just mushy potato strings. \n\nAnyway... what did you ask? Worrying? No, I don't really worry about what's going to happen. I just don't really care enough to worry, I guess. It's just one day after another.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don't know, maybe. I don't really do much anymore. I used to like building those little model airplanes on the weekends. The ones from World War II, mostly. I had this really cool P-51 Mustang kit that came with authentic decals and everything. The glue for those things is awful, though. It smells like nail polish remover and if you get it on your fingers, it dries into this weird crust that takes days to peel off. My cousin used to sniff that stuff when we were kids, which explains a lot about him now, honestly. He moved out to Arizona a few years ago to start an emu farm. Emus! Can you imagine? They're basically dinosaurs. I saw a video once of an emu chasing a guy on a bicycle and it was terrifying. They have those massive claws on their feet. I'd never want to live in Arizona anyway, it's way too hot. And you have to check your shoes for scorpions every morning. Imagine waking up, putting your foot in your sneaker, and getting stung by a scorpion. I only buy sneakers with velcro now anyway, because bending down to tie laces just feels like too much effort. \n\nWait, what was the question? Lost interest? Yeah, I don't really build the planes anymore. I just sit on the couch. Nothing really sounds fun.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight changes? Um, no, not really. My weight's been pretty much the same. I mean, my pants still fit exactly like they always do. I wear those dark blue work pants, you know, the ones with the reinforced knees? They're supposed to be stain-resistant, but bleach still ruins them. I spilled a whole bottle of bleach last month in the mop closet because the shelf bracket just gave out. The maintenance guy, Frank, he used drywall anchors instead of finding the stud when he put it up. Who does that for a shelf holding liquid chemicals? Frank’s always cutting corners like that. He drives this old beat-up Chevy truck that makes a terrible squealing noise every time he turns the steering wheel. Power steering fluid leak, probably. I had a car that did that once, a '98 Honda Civic. Great gas mileage, but the heater core blew in the middle of January. Have you ever driven on the highway with no heat in January? I had to wear my winter coat and a ski mask just to commute. I looked like I was about to rob a bank at 6 AM. The toll booth guy actually looked terrified when I handed him my money. \n\nAnyway... what did you ask? Weight? Yeah, no, my weight hasn't changed. Same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? I don't know, I guess I just don't feel much of anything. It's like everything is just kind of gray, you know? Even things that used to be okay, like watching the game on Sunday. I used to watch football every week. My dad was a huge fan, he had this lucky jersey that he never washed. It smelled like old beer and stale cigarette smoke. He used to smoke those unfiltered cigarettes, the ones that come in the soft pack. I never understood how he could smoke those, they just seem so harsh. I tried one once when I was a teenager and coughed for like ten minutes straight. My throat felt like I swallowed sandpaper. You know what else feels like sandpaper? The cheap toilet paper they stock in the building where I work. It's that single-ply stuff that practically dissolves if you look at it wrong. The tenants complain about it all the time, but management won't spring for the good stuff. They're always trying to save a few pennies here and there. Like last week, they switched the soap dispensers from the foaming kind to this weird pink liquid that smells like bubblegum and hospital floors. It's so gross. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Pleasure? Yeah, I don't really get any happiness out of things. I just kind of exist, I guess. Nothing really matters that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel nervous or on edge most of the time?\n\nPatient: Nervous? On edge? No, not really. I don't get worked up about much. I'm too tired to be nervous, honestly. It takes energy to be on edge, you know? Like my neighbor's dog, this little terrier mix. That thing is always on edge. It barks at every single leaf that blows past the window. Yap, yap, yap, all day long. The owner, she's this older lady who always wears these bright floral muumuus, even in the middle of winter. She says the dog has anxiety and needs a special weighted blanket. A blanket for a dog! Can you believe that? I saw her carrying it into the elevator once. It looked like a miniature sleeping bag. Elevators are the worst place to get stuck with someone who wants to talk, especially when you're just trying to finish your shift and go home. The service elevator in my building is so slow, it takes a solid two minutes just to get from the basement to the lobby. And it makes this awful groaning sound halfway up, like the cables are about to snap. I told management about it, but they just told me to keep the floors buffed and stop worrying about the mechanics. I use this industrial buffer, it's heavy as hell. If you don't hold it just right, it'll swing you right into the wall. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Nervous? Oh, right. No, I don't feel nervous. I'm just... flat. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: Worrying about the future? Um, no, I don't really think about it. I mean, what's there to worry about? It's just gonna be the same as today, and yesterday. Just... more of the same. I don't really look forward to anything, so I don't worry about it either. It's like looking at a blank wall. Speaking of walls, the paint they use in the hallways at work is this awful beige color. They call it \"Navajo White,\" but it's not white at all. It's like the color of old oatmeal. And it shows every single scuff mark. People are always dragging their luggage or their bikes against it, and I'm the one who has to scrub it off with a Magic Eraser. Those things are amazing, by the way. I don't know what's in them, probably some kind of toxic chemical, but they take off everything. I tried using one on my sneakers once, these white New Balances I got on sale. It worked pretty well, but it kind of took the finish off the leather. Now they get dirty twice as fast. You ever try to keep white shoes clean in the winter? It's impossible. The salt on the sidewalks just eats right through the material. The city uses this cheap rock salt that's practically boulders. You step on it and it feels like you're walking on gravel. \n\nUh, sorry, what was the question? Worrying? Yeah, no. I don't worry about the future. I just don't really care what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Didn't you just ask me that? Or maybe that was someone else. I don't know, it all blurs together. But yeah, no pleasure. Like I said, everything's just gray. It's like watching a TV with the color turned all the way down. I used to have this old tube TV, you know, the big heavy ones that took two people to lift? I found it on the curb one trash day. People throw away perfectly good stuff all the time. At the building, I see it constantly. Last month, someone tossed out a whole set of golf clubs. Just left them by the dumpster. They were in this nice leather bag, too. I thought about taking them, maybe trying to learn how to play, but then I figured, when would I have the time? Or the energy? Plus, I don't even like being outside that much. The sun gives me a headache. And the golf courses around here are so expensive anyway. You have to pay just to walk on the grass. Grass is a pain to maintain, too. The landscaping crew at work is always complaining about the sprinkler system. They say the timer is busted and it goes off at 3 AM, flooding the flower beds. They planted these petunias that look like they're drowning half the time. \n\nAnyway, what was it? Pleasure? Yeah, no. I don't feel anything like that. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel fearful or apprehensive about everyday situations?\n\nPatient: Fearful? Like, scared of things? No, not really. I don't get scared of everyday stuff. It's just... I'm just here, you know? Like I'm just going through the motions. There's no fear, just a lot of dragging myself around. Speaking of dragging, the vacuum cleaner I use for the lobby carpets is an absolute nightmare. It's this huge, industrial thing with a cord that must be fifty feet long. I swear it weighs as much as a small car. The wheels on it are squeaky, too. Squeak, squeak, squeak, all the way down the hall. I tried putting WD-40 on them, but it didn't do a thing. I think the bearings are shot. You ever try to fix something and it just makes it worse? Like last week, the sink in my apartment was dripping. Just a slow drip, but it was driving me crazy at night. Drip... drip... drip. So, I got under there with a wrench, figured I could just tighten it up. Well, I tightened it alright, and the whole pipe cracked. Water everywhere. Had to call the super, and he was not happy about it. He's this guy named Frank, always wears these stained undershirts and smells like stale cigars. He spent an hour under my sink complaining about how they don't make pipes like they used to. He said it was all cheap PVC now, not like the good old copper pipes. Copper's expensive, though. People steal it out of abandoned buildings all the time. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Fearful? Yeah, no. I'm not scared of anything. I just don't have the energy for it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Uneasy? Um, no, not really. I don't get that feeling of apprehension or whatever. Like I said, it's just... flat. Everything's just flat. I don't get worked up over things that aren't there. I barely get worked up over the things that *are* there. Like the trash bins outside the building. They're these huge metal dumpsters, and the lids are always getting stuck. You have to heave them open, and half the time they slam back down on your hand. I've got this bruise on my knuckles right now from Tuesday. It's turning that weird yellowish-green color. It's funny how bruises do that, right? Like a mood ring, but for injuries. I had one on my shin once that looked exactly like a map of Florida. I got it from walking into the coffee table in the dark. My apartment has terrible lighting. The fixtures are all from the seventies, those ugly frosted glass domes that make everything look yellow. I tried changing the bulbs to those daylight LEDs, but then it just looked like a hospital room. Too bright, you know? It gave me a headache. I ended up just keeping the lights off most of the time anyway. It's cheaper on the electric bill, and it's not like I'm reading or doing anything that needs good light. 

So, um, apprehension? No. I don't really feel that. Just... tired, mostly.

Those big office buildings are so weird at night, by the way. Like, they have these motion-sensor lights in the hallways, but half of them are on a delay, so you’re just pushing your mop bucket into pitch blackness for a solid ten feet before they finally click on. It drives me crazy. And the security guy at the main building, Frank, he just sits at the front desk watching golf on his phone all night. Doesn't even do his rounds, just sits there spitting sunflower seeds into a cup. But yeah, ever since they messed with our schedules three years ago, I just haven't been able to keep my head straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Depressed? Yeah, I guess so. Most days I just feel like there’s this... this heavy, flat feeling following me around. Like, even when I’m just pushing my cleaning cart down an empty hallway at work. Which, by the way, the front left wheel on my cart has been jammed for like a month. I put in a work order, but maintenance is totally dragging their feet. They use this cheap supplier from two towns over because the property manager's brother-in-law owns it or something. Total conflict of interest, if you ask me. \n\nBut yeah, I just feel heavy all the time. Even when I’m at home, sitting with my family, I'm just trying to act normal for them. We were all sitting around watching that new baking show the other night—the one where they make cakes that look like real objects? My wife loves that stuff. She actually tried making a cake that looked like a sneaker once, but the fondant got all weird and sweaty. It tasted awful, honestly, like pure sugar paste. But anyway... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, just a flat, heavy feeling most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious? I mean, not really. I don't get panicky or anything like that. Well, I guess I get a little worked up when the regional managers do their surprise walk-throughs at the buildings. They always send this guy, Peterson, who walks around with a clipboard and a little LED flashlight, checking the hinges on the emergency doors and looking for dust on the baseboards. He drives this bright yellow Prius, which... honestly, who buys a yellow car on purpose? \n\nMy uncle had a yellow truck once, an old Chevy, but he actually painted it himself in his garage. He didn't tape anything off right, so he got yellow paint all over his riding lawnmower. It had this big weird stripe down the side of the engine for years, and he just refused to scrape it off. He said it gave the mower character. He was a really stubborn guy, my uncle. Always wore these thick suspenders even with sweatpants. But anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, anxiety. No, I don't really feel nervous or anything. Just that flat feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Enjoying things? I mean... I don't really know. I guess I haven't really thought about it. I used to like going fishing out at the state park, but I haven't been in a long time. My buddy Mark actually just bought a new boat for that lake, though. It’s one of those big pontoon boats with the fake leather seats? They get ridiculously hot in the sun. He actually burned the back of his legs on them last weekend because he was wearing these super short cargo shorts. I told him he looked like an 80s gym teacher. You know the type, with the whistle on the lanyard, always yelling at kids to climb that stupid thick rope in the gymnasium. Man, I hated that rope climb when I was a kid. The crash mat underneath it always smelled exactly like old cheese and sweaty socks, and it had this weird silver duct tape patch right in the middle where somebody ripped it. They never replaced anything at that school. Just slapped tape on it and called it a day. \n\nBut, uh... sorry, what was the question? Enjoying stuff? Yeah, I'm not really sure. I just go to work and come home, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like fishing?\n\nPatient: Pleasure in fishing? I mean, I guess I just don't have the energy to even pack up the gear anymore. It's a whole process, you know? You gotta check the lines, make sure the tackle box isn't a mess. Which, last time I opened mine, I found this ancient pack of beef jerky in there. God knows how long it had been sitting at the bottom under the sinkers. It was hard as a rock. Reminded me of this time my dad tried to make his own jerky in the oven when I was a teenager. He left it in for like twelve hours on the lowest setting, and the whole house smelled like liquid smoke and burnt soy sauce for a week. My mom was so mad she made him sleep on the couch, which was this awful floral pattern thing from the 70s. It had these scratchy threads that would leave red marks on your face if you laid on it wrong. We ended up leaving that couch on the curb when we moved to the apartment on 4th street. The garbage guys wouldn't even take it, so it just sat there getting rained on until some college kids hauled it away in a pickup truck. \n\nBut anyway... what were you asking? Oh, right. Fishing. Yeah, I don't know, I just... I don't really get any enjoyment out of the idea of it right now. I just kind of exist, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... I don't think so. Not really anything specific to feel guilty about, I guess. I just go to work and do my job. Although, I did feel a little bad the other day when I accidentally knocked over Mrs. Higgins' spider plant while I was mopping the hallway on the third floor. It was one of those big, bushy ones in a heavy ceramic pot. The pot didn't break, thank God, but dirt went everywhere. It took me like twenty minutes to sweep it all up out of the carpet. Carpet in an office building is such a terrible idea anyway. It holds onto every single stain. There's this one coffee stain near the elevator that's been there since I started working here five years ago. I've tried everything on it—industrial cleaner, baking soda, even this weird foam stuff my supervisor ordered from a late-night infomercial. The guy on the TV was shouting about how it could lift red wine out of a white shag rug, but it didn't do squat for that coffee stain. It just made the hallway smell like artificial pine needles and chemicals for three days. \n\nWait, what was the question? Guilt? No, I don't really feel guilty about stuff. I'm just kind of... there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because you feel overwhelmed or stressed by them?\n\nPatient: Avoiding things? I mean, I still get the cleaning done at work, but I’m definitely a lot slower. I just second-guess my logs so much lately that I’ve actually stopped pushing for the supervisor track. Being a supervisor means carrying around that giant metal clipboard anyway. Have you seen those things? They weigh like five pounds and the spring clip always snaps down on your fingers if you aren't careful. My old boss, Dave, he actually lost a fingernail to one of those clips a few years ago. It just turned completely black and fell off in the middle of a shift while he was eating a turkey sandwich. Grossest thing I ever saw. He was eating it on this weird rye bread that had those little seeds in it—caraway seeds, I think? I hate those. They get stuck in your teeth for days. I had one stuck in my molar once right before a family get-together... which, actually, I've been skipping a lot of those lately. Family stuff, I mean. And those neighborhood clean-ups I used to make time for. I just... skip them. My aunt always makes this potato salad for the family things anyway, and she puts raisins in it. Who puts raisins in potato salad? It's a crime against mayonnaise, honestly. She says it adds sweet texture, but it just feels like you're chewing on a tick or something. \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Overwhelmed? Yeah, I guess I just avoid the extra stuff now, like the supervisor track or seeing family. I just second-guess myself too much to deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about the future or things that might happen?\n\nPatient: Worry about the future? I don't know, not really. I mean, I don't really think about the future much. It just seems like a lot of work to think that far ahead. Speaking of work, the floor buffer broke again yesterday. The cord got all tangled up around the wheels. It's one of those bright yellow cords, you know the ones? They’re supposed to be heavy-duty, but they always get those weird kinks in them if you don't wrap them up perfectly in a figure-eight pattern. My old roommate used to wrap his guitar cables like that. He was in this terrible garage band that practiced on Tuesdays. Why Tuesdays? I have no idea. But they played this awful cover of \"Free Bird\" that went on for like twenty minutes, and the bassist was always out of tune. He had this bass that was painted neon green with black splatters, like some kind of toxic waste barrel. He bought it at a pawn shop off the highway, the one next to that diner that serves breakfast 24/7. I went to that diner once and ordered pancakes, but they came out tasting like onions because they didn't clean the grill after making hash browns. Hash browns are only good if they're crispy anyway, otherwise it's just mushy potato strings. \n\nAnyway... what did you ask? Worrying? No, I don't really worry about what's going to happen. I just don't really care enough to worry, I guess. It's just one day after another.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don't know, maybe. I don't really do much anymore. I used to like building those little model airplanes on the weekends. The ones from World War II, mostly. I had this really cool P-51 Mustang kit that came with authentic decals and everything. The glue for those things is awful, though. It smells like nail polish remover and if you get it on your fingers, it dries into this weird crust that takes days to peel off. My cousin used to sniff that stuff when we were kids, which explains a lot about him now, honestly. He moved out to Arizona a few years ago to start an emu farm. Emus! Can you imagine? They're basically dinosaurs. I saw a video once of an emu chasing a guy on a bicycle and it was terrifying. They have those massive claws on their feet. I'd never want to live in Arizona anyway, it's way too hot. And you have to check your shoes for scorpions every morning. Imagine waking up, putting your foot in your sneaker, and getting stung by a scorpion. I only buy sneakers with velcro now anyway, because bending down to tie laces just feels like too much effort. \n\nWait, what was the question? Lost interest? Yeah, I don't really build the planes anymore. I just sit on the couch. Nothing really sounds fun.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight changes? Um, no, not really. My weight's been pretty much the same. I mean, my pants still fit exactly like they always do. I wear those dark blue work pants, you know, the ones with the reinforced knees? They're supposed to be stain-resistant, but bleach still ruins them. I spilled a whole bottle of bleach last month in the mop closet because the shelf bracket just gave out. The maintenance guy, Frank, he used drywall anchors instead of finding the stud when he put it up. Who does that for a shelf holding liquid chemicals? Frank’s always cutting corners like that. He drives this old beat-up Chevy truck that makes a terrible squealing noise every time he turns the steering wheel. Power steering fluid leak, probably. I had a car that did that once, a '98 Honda Civic. Great gas mileage, but the heater core blew in the middle of January. Have you ever driven on the highway with no heat in January? I had to wear my winter coat and a ski mask just to commute. I looked like I was about to rob a bank at 6 AM. The toll booth guy actually looked terrified when I handed him my money. \n\nAnyway... what did you ask? Weight? Yeah, no, my weight hasn't changed. Same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? I don't know, I guess I just don't feel much of anything. It's like everything is just kind of gray, you know? Even things that used to be okay, like watching the game on Sunday. I used to watch football every week. My dad was a huge fan, he had this lucky jersey that he never washed. It smelled like old beer and stale cigarette smoke. He used to smoke those unfiltered cigarettes, the ones that come in the soft pack. I never understood how he could smoke those, they just seem so harsh. I tried one once when I was a teenager and coughed for like ten minutes straight. My throat felt like I swallowed sandpaper. You know what else feels like sandpaper? The cheap toilet paper they stock in the building where I work. It's that single-ply stuff that practically dissolves if you look at it wrong. The tenants complain about it all the time, but management won't spring for the good stuff. They're always trying to save a few pennies here and there. Like last week, they switched the soap dispensers from the foaming kind to this weird pink liquid that smells like bubblegum and hospital floors. It's so gross. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Pleasure? Yeah, I don't really get any happiness out of things. I just kind of exist, I guess. Nothing really matters that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel nervous or on edge most of the time?\n\nPatient: Nervous? On edge? No, not really. I don't get worked up about much. I'm too tired to be nervous, honestly. It takes energy to be on edge, you know? Like my neighbor's dog, this little terrier mix. That thing is always on edge. It barks at every single leaf that blows past the window. Yap, yap, yap, all day long. The owner, she's this older lady who always wears these bright floral muumuus, even in the middle of winter. She says the dog has anxiety and needs a special weighted blanket. A blanket for a dog! Can you believe that? I saw her carrying it into the elevator once. It looked like a miniature sleeping bag. Elevators are the worst place to get stuck with someone who wants to talk, especially when you're just trying to finish your shift and go home. The service elevator in my building is so slow, it takes a solid two minutes just to get from the basement to the lobby. And it makes this awful groaning sound halfway up, like the cables are about to snap. I told management about it, but they just told me to keep the floors buffed and stop worrying about the mechanics. I use this industrial buffer, it's heavy as hell. If you don't hold it just right, it'll swing you right into the wall. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Nervous? Oh, right. No, I don't feel nervous. I'm just... flat. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: Worrying about the future? Um, no, I don't really think about it. I mean, what's there to worry about? It's just gonna be the same as today, and yesterday. Just... more of the same. I don't really look forward to anything, so I don't worry about it either. It's like looking at a blank wall. Speaking of walls, the paint they use in the hallways at work is this awful beige color. They call it \"Navajo White,\" but it's not white at all. It's like the color of old oatmeal. And it shows every single scuff mark. People are always dragging their luggage or their bikes against it, and I'm the one who has to scrub it off with a Magic Eraser. Those things are amazing, by the way. I don't know what's in them, probably some kind of toxic chemical, but they take off everything. I tried using one on my sneakers once, these white New Balances I got on sale. It worked pretty well, but it kind of took the finish off the leather. Now they get dirty twice as fast. You ever try to keep white shoes clean in the winter? It's impossible. The salt on the sidewalks just eats right through the material. The city uses this cheap rock salt that's practically boulders. You step on it and it feels like you're walking on gravel. \n\nUh, sorry, what was the question? Worrying? Yeah, no. I don't worry about the future. I just don't really care what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Didn't you just ask me that? Or maybe that was someone else. I don't know, it all blurs together. But yeah, no pleasure. Like I said, everything's just gray. It's like watching a TV with the color turned all the way down. I used to have this old tube TV, you know, the big heavy ones that took two people to lift? I found it on the curb one trash day. People throw away perfectly good stuff all the time. At the building, I see it constantly. Last month, someone tossed out a whole set of golf clubs. Just left them by the dumpster. They were in this nice leather bag, too. I thought about taking them, maybe trying to learn how to play, but then I figured, when would I have the time? Or the energy? Plus, I don't even like being outside that much. The sun gives me a headache. And the golf courses around here are so expensive anyway. You have to pay just to walk on the grass. Grass is a pain to maintain, too. The landscaping crew at work is always complaining about the sprinkler system. They say the timer is busted and it goes off at 3 AM, flooding the flower beds. They planted these petunias that look like they're drowning half the time. \n\nAnyway, what was it? Pleasure? Yeah, no. I don't feel anything like that. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel fearful or apprehensive about everyday situations?\n\nPatient: Fearful? Like, scared of things? No, not really. I don't get scared of everyday stuff. It's just... I'm just here, you know? Like I'm just going through the motions. There's no fear, just a lot of dragging myself around. Speaking of dragging, the vacuum cleaner I use for the lobby carpets is an absolute nightmare. It's this huge, industrial thing with a cord that must be fifty feet long. I swear it weighs as much as a small car. The wheels on it are squeaky, too. Squeak, squeak, squeak, all the way down the hall. I tried putting WD-40 on them, but it didn't do a thing. I think the bearings are shot. You ever try to fix something and it just makes it worse? Like last week, the sink in my apartment was dripping. Just a slow drip, but it was driving me crazy at night. Drip... drip... drip. So, I got under there with a wrench, figured I could just tighten it up. Well, I tightened it alright, and the whole pipe cracked. Water everywhere. Had to call the super, and he was not happy about it. He's this guy named Frank, always wears these stained undershirts and smells like stale cigars. He spent an hour under my sink complaining about how they don't make pipes like they used to. He said it was all cheap PVC now, not like the good old copper pipes. Copper's expensive, though. People steal it out of abandoned buildings all the time. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Fearful? Yeah, no. I'm not scared of anything. I just don't have the energy for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel uneasy or have a sense of apprehension that persists even when there's no apparent reason for it?\n\nPatient: Uneasy? Um, no, not really. I don't get that feeling of apprehension or whatever. Like I said, it's just... flat. Everything's just flat. I don't get worked up over things that aren't there. I barely get worked up over the things that *are* there. Like the trash bins outside the building. They're these huge metal dumpsters, and the lids are always getting stuck. You have to heave them open, and half the time they slam back down on your hand. I've got this bruise on my knuckles right now from Tuesday. It's turning that weird yellowish-green color. It's funny how bruises do that, right? Like a mood ring, but for injuries. I had one on my shin once that looked exactly like a map of Florida. I got it from walking into the coffee table in the dark. My apartment has terrible lighting. The fixtures are all from the seventies, those ugly frosted glass domes that make everything look yellow. I tried changing the bulbs to those daylight LEDs, but then it just looked like a hospital room. Too bright, you know? It gave me a headache. I ended up just keeping the lights off most of the time anyway. It's cheaper on the electric bill, and it's not like I'm reading or doing anything that needs good light. \n\nSo, um, apprehension? No. I don't really feel that. Just... tired, mostly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But they played this awful cover of \"Free Bird\" that went on for like twenty minutes, and the bassist was always out of tune. He had this bass that was painted neon green with black splatters, like some kind of toxic waste barrel. He bought it at a pawn shop off the highway, the one next to that diner that serves breakfast 24/7. I went to that diner once and ordered pancakes, but they came out tasting like onions because they didn't clean the grill after making hash browns. Hash browns are only good if they're crispy anyway, otherwise it's just mushy potato strings. \n\nAnyway... what did you ask? Worrying? No, I don't really worry about what's going to happen. I just don't really care enough to worry, I guess. It's just one day after another.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don't know, maybe. I don't really do much anymore. I used to like building those little model airplanes on the weekends. The ones from World War II, mostly. I had this really cool P-51 Mustang kit that came with authentic decals and everything. The glue for those things is awful, though. It smells like nail polish remover and if you get it on your fingers, it dries into this weird crust that takes days to peel off. My cousin used to sniff that stuff when we were kids, which explains a lot about him now, honestly. He moved out to Arizona a few years ago to start an emu farm. Emus! Can you imagine? They're basically dinosaurs. I saw a video once of an emu chasing a guy on a bicycle and it was terrifying. They have those massive claws on their feet. I'd never want to live in Arizona anyway, it's way too hot. And you have to check your shoes for scorpions every morning. Imagine waking up, putting your foot in your sneaker, and getting stung by a scorpion. I only buy sneakers with velcro now anyway, because bending down to tie laces just feels like too much effort. \n\nWait, what was the question? Lost interest? Yeah, I don't really build the planes anymore. I just sit on the couch. Nothing really sounds fun.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight changes? Um, no, not really. My weight's been pretty much the same. I mean, my pants still fit exactly like they always do. I wear those dark blue work pants, you know, the ones with the reinforced knees? They're supposed to be stain-resistant, but bleach still ruins them. I spilled a whole bottle of bleach last month in the mop closet because the shelf bracket just gave out. The maintenance guy, Frank, he used drywall anchors instead of finding the stud when he put it up. Who does that for a shelf holding liquid chemicals? Frank’s always cutting corners like that. He drives this old beat-up Chevy truck that makes a terrible squealing noise every time he turns the steering wheel. Power steering fluid leak, probably. I had a car that did that once, a '98 Honda Civic. Great gas mileage, but the heater core blew in the middle of January. Have you ever driven on the highway with no heat in January? I had to wear my winter coat and a ski mask just to commute. I looked like I was about to rob a bank at 6 AM. The toll booth guy actually looked terrified when I handed him my money. \n\nAnyway... what did you ask? Weight? Yeah, no, my weight hasn't changed. Same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? I don't know, I guess I just don't feel much of anything. It's like everything is just kind of gray, you know? Even things that used to be okay, like watching the game on Sunday. I used to watch football every week. My dad was a huge fan, he had this lucky jersey that he never washed. It smelled like old beer and stale cigarette smoke. He used to smoke those unfiltered cigarettes, the ones that come in the soft pack. I never understood how he could smoke those, they just seem so harsh. I tried one once when I was a teenager and coughed for like ten minutes straight. My throat felt like I swallowed sandpaper. You know what else feels like sandpaper? The cheap toilet paper they stock in the building where I work. It's that single-ply stuff that practically dissolves if you look at it wrong. The tenants complain about it all the time, but management won't spring for the good stuff. They're always trying to save a few pennies here and there. Like last week, they switched the soap dispensers from the foaming kind to this weird pink liquid that smells like bubblegum and hospital floors. It's so gross. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Pleasure? Yeah, I don't really get any happiness out of things. I just kind of exist, I guess. Nothing really matters that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel nervous or on edge most of the time?\n\nPatient: Nervous? On edge? No, not really. I don't get worked up about much. I'm too tired to be nervous, honestly. It takes energy to be on edge, you know? Like my neighbor's dog, this little terrier mix. That thing is always on edge. It barks at every single leaf that blows past the window. Yap, yap, yap, all day long. The owner, she's this older lady who always wears these bright floral muumuus, even in the middle of winter. She says the dog has anxiety and needs a special weighted blanket. A blanket for a dog! Can you believe that? I saw her carrying it into the elevator once. It looked like a miniature sleeping bag. Elevators are the worst place to get stuck with someone who wants to talk, especially when you're just trying to finish your shift and go home. The service elevator in my building is so slow, it takes a solid two minutes just to get from the basement to the lobby. And it makes this awful groaning sound halfway up, like the cables are about to snap. I told management about it, but they just told me to keep the floors buffed and stop worrying about the mechanics. I use this industrial buffer, it's heavy as hell. If you don't hold it just right, it'll swing you right into the wall. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Nervous? Oh, right. No, I don't feel nervous. I'm just... flat. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: Worrying about the future? Um, no, I don't really think about it. I mean, what's there to worry about? It's just gonna be the same as today, and yesterday. Just... more of the same. I don't really look forward to anything, so I don't worry about it either. It's like looking at a blank wall. Speaking of walls, the paint they use in the hallways at work is this awful beige color. They call it \"Navajo White,\" but it's not white at all. It's like the color of old oatmeal. And it shows every single scuff mark. People are always dragging their luggage or their bikes against it, and I'm the one who has to scrub it off with a Magic Eraser. Those things are amazing, by the way. I don't know what's in them, probably some kind of toxic chemical, but they take off everything. I tried using one on my sneakers once, these white New Balances I got on sale. It worked pretty well, but it kind of took the finish off the leather. Now they get dirty twice as fast. You ever try to keep white shoes clean in the winter? It's impossible. The salt on the sidewalks just eats right through the material. The city uses this cheap rock salt that's practically boulders. You step on it and it feels like you're walking on gravel. \n\nUh, sorry, what was the question? Worrying? Yeah, no. I don't worry about the future. I just don't really care what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Didn't you just ask me that? Or maybe that was someone else. I don't know, it all blurs together. But yeah, no pleasure. Like I said, everything's just gray. It's like watching a TV with the color turned all the way down. I used to have this old tube TV, you know, the big heavy ones that took two people to lift? I found it on the curb one trash day. People throw away perfectly good stuff all the time. At the building, I see it constantly. Last month, someone tossed out a whole set of golf clubs. Just left them by the dumpster. They were in this nice leather bag, too. I thought about taking them, maybe trying to learn how to play, but then I figured, when would I have the time? Or the energy? Plus, I don't even like being outside that much. The sun gives me a headache. And the golf courses around here are so expensive anyway. You have to pay just to walk on the grass. Grass is a pain to maintain, too. The landscaping crew at work is always complaining about the sprinkler system. They say the timer is busted and it goes off at 3 AM, flooding the flower beds. They planted these petunias that look like they're drowning half the time. \n\nAnyway, what was it? Pleasure? Yeah, no. I don't feel anything like that. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel fearful or apprehensive about everyday situations?\n\nPatient: Fearful? Like, scared of things? No, not really. I don't get scared of everyday stuff. It's just... I'm just here, you know? Like I'm just going through the motions. There's no fear, just a lot of dragging myself around. Speaking of dragging, the vacuum cleaner I use for the lobby carpets is an absolute nightmare. It's this huge, industrial thing with a cord that must be fifty feet long. I swear it weighs as much as a small car. The wheels on it are squeaky, too. Squeak, squeak, squeak, all the way down the hall. I tried putting WD-40 on them, but it didn't do a thing. I think the bearings are shot. You ever try to fix something and it just makes it worse? Like last week, the sink in my apartment was dripping. Just a slow drip, but it was driving me crazy at night. Drip... drip... drip. So, I got under there with a wrench, figured I could just tighten it up. Well, I tightened it alright, and the whole pipe cracked. Water everywhere. Had to call the super, and he was not happy about it. He's this guy named Frank, always wears these stained undershirts and smells like stale cigars. He spent an hour under my sink complaining about how they don't make pipes like they used to. He said it was all cheap PVC now, not like the good old copper pipes. Copper's expensive, though. People steal it out of abandoned buildings all the time. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Fearful? Yeah, no. I'm not scared of anything. I just don't have the energy for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel uneasy or have a sense of apprehension that persists even when there's no apparent reason for it?\n\nPatient: Uneasy? Um, no, not really. I don't get that feeling of apprehension or whatever. Like I said, it's just... flat. Everything's just flat. I don't get worked up over things that aren't there. I barely get worked up over the things that *are* there. Like the trash bins outside the building. They're these huge metal dumpsters, and the lids are always getting stuck. You have to heave them open, and half the time they slam back down on your hand. I've got this bruise on my knuckles right now from Tuesday. It's turning that weird yellowish-green color. It's funny how bruises do that, right? Like a mood ring, but for injuries. I had one on my shin once that looked exactly like a map of Florida. I got it from walking into the coffee table in the dark. My apartment has terrible lighting. The fixtures are all from the seventies, those ugly frosted glass domes that make everything look yellow. I tried changing the bulbs to those daylight LEDs, but then it just looked like a hospital room. Too bright, you know? It gave me a headache. I ended up just keeping the lights off most of the time anyway. It's cheaper on the electric bill, and it's not like I'm reading or doing anything that needs good light. \n\nSo, um, apprehension? No. I don't really feel that. Just... tired, mostly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Changes? Over the past few months? I mean, not really changes, no. It's just... it's been the same for a long time. Most days I feel like there's this heavy, flat feeling following me around, you know? Even when I'm just pushing the cleaning cart down an empty hallway at work. The wheels on that cart, man, they're almost as bad as the vacuum cleaner. One of them is completely seized up, so you have to kind of shove it sideways to get it to turn a corner. It leaves these black scuff marks on the linoleum that I then have to go back and buff out. It's like I'm creating more work for myself just by doing my job. I asked the building manager for a new cart back in October, but he said it wasn't in the budget. Everything's always "not in the budget." But they somehow had the budget to buy these giant, ugly modern art sculptures for the lobby. They look like twisted up paperclips. People are always leaving their coffee cups on the base of them, like they're tables. Then I have to clean up the coffee rings. 

And even when I'm not at work, like if I'm sitting with my family and trying to act normal, that heavy feeling is just... there. It doesn't go away. So, no, not really any changes. Just the same heavy flat thing.

Dave's always saying stuff like that, though. He bought this old boat last summer and spent three months trying to fix the motor before realizing it was completely rusted out. Anyway, I keep reading the same line in the manual, or I'll walk all the way down to the supply closet on the third floor and completely forget what I went in there for. The third floor is always freezing, by the way, I think the vents are messed up. But yeah, I just can't seem to focus on anything.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having trouble staying focused?\n\nPatient: Man, it feels like forever, but... probably about three years? Yeah, three years. That’s when the contractor switched everything up on us and put us on these rotating evening and overnight shifts. They moved us to the larger properties, too. Those big office buildings are so weird at night, by the way. Like, they have these motion-sensor lights in the hallways, but half of them are on a delay, so you’re just pushing your mop bucket into pitch blackness for a solid ten feet before they finally click on. It drives me crazy. And the security guy at the main building, Frank, he just sits at the front desk watching golf on his phone all night. Doesn't even do his rounds, just sits there spitting sunflower seeds into a cup. But yeah, ever since they messed with our schedules three years ago, I just haven't been able to keep my head straight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Depressed? Yeah, I guess so. Most days I just feel like there’s this... this heavy, flat feeling following me around. Like, even when I’m just pushing my cleaning cart down an empty hallway at work. Which, by the way, the front left wheel on my cart has been jammed for like a month. I put in a work order, but maintenance is totally dragging their feet. They use this cheap supplier from two towns over because the property manager's brother-in-law owns it or something. Total conflict of interest, if you ask me. \n\nBut yeah, I just feel heavy all the time. Even when I’m at home, sitting with my family, I'm just trying to act normal for them. We were all sitting around watching that new baking show the other night—the one where they make cakes that look like real objects? My wife loves that stuff. She actually tried making a cake that looked like a sneaker once, but the fondant got all weird and sweaty. It tasted awful, honestly, like pure sugar paste. But anyway... what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, just a flat, heavy feeling most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious? I mean, not really. I don't get panicky or anything like that. Well, I guess I get a little worked up when the regional managers do their surprise walk-throughs at the buildings. They always send this guy, Peterson, who walks around with a clipboard and a little LED flashlight, checking the hinges on the emergency doors and looking for dust on the baseboards. He drives this bright yellow Prius, which... honestly, who buys a yellow car on purpose? \n\nMy uncle had a yellow truck once, an old Chevy, but he actually painted it himself in his garage. He didn't tape anything off right, so he got yellow paint all over his riding lawnmower. It had this big weird stripe down the side of the engine for years, and he just refused to scrape it off. He said it gave the mower character. He was a really stubborn guy, my uncle. Always wore these thick suspenders even with sweatpants. But anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, anxiety. No, I don't really feel nervous or anything. Just that flat feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Enjoying things? I mean... I don't really know. I guess I haven't really thought about it. I used to like going fishing out at the state park, but I haven't been in a long time. My buddy Mark actually just bought a new boat for that lake, though. It’s one of those big pontoon boats with the fake leather seats? They get ridiculously hot in the sun. He actually burned the back of his legs on them last weekend because he was wearing these super short cargo shorts. I told him he looked like an 80s gym teacher. You know the type, with the whistle on the lanyard, always yelling at kids to climb that stupid thick rope in the gymnasium. Man, I hated that rope climb when I was a kid. The crash mat underneath it always smelled exactly like old cheese and sweaty socks, and it had this weird silver duct tape patch right in the middle where somebody ripped it. They never replaced anything at that school. Just slapped tape on it and called it a day. \n\nBut, uh... sorry, what was the question? Enjoying stuff? Yeah, I'm not really sure. I just go to work and come home, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like fishing?\n\nPatient: Pleasure in fishing? I mean, I guess I just don't have the energy to even pack up the gear anymore. It's a whole process, you know? You gotta check the lines, make sure the tackle box isn't a mess. Which, last time I opened mine, I found this ancient pack of beef jerky in there. God knows how long it had been sitting at the bottom under the sinkers. It was hard as a rock. Reminded me of this time my dad tried to make his own jerky in the oven when I was a teenager. He left it in for like twelve hours on the lowest setting, and the whole house smelled like liquid smoke and burnt soy sauce for a week. My mom was so mad she made him sleep on the couch, which was this awful floral pattern thing from the 70s. It had these scratchy threads that would leave red marks on your face if you laid on it wrong. We ended up leaving that couch on the curb when we moved to the apartment on 4th street. The garbage guys wouldn't even take it, so it just sat there getting rained on until some college kids hauled it away in a pickup truck. \n\nBut anyway... what were you asking? Oh, right. Fishing. Yeah, I don't know, I just... I don't really get any enjoyment out of the idea of it right now. I just kind of exist, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... I don't think so. Not really anything specific to feel guilty about, I guess. I just go to work and do my job. Although, I did feel a little bad the other day when I accidentally knocked over Mrs. Higgins' spider plant while I was mopping the hallway on the third floor. It was one of those big, bushy ones in a heavy ceramic pot. The pot didn't break, thank God, but dirt went everywhere. It took me like twenty minutes to sweep it all up out of the carpet. Carpet in an office building is such a terrible idea anyway. It holds onto every single stain. There's this one coffee stain near the elevator that's been there since I started working here five years ago. I've tried everything on it—industrial cleaner, baking soda, even this weird foam stuff my supervisor ordered from a late-night infomercial. The guy on the TV was shouting about how it could lift red wine out of a white shag rug, but it didn't do squat for that coffee stain. It just made the hallway smell like artificial pine needles and chemicals for three days. \n\nWait, what was the question? Guilt? No, I don't really feel guilty about stuff. I'm just kind of... there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because you feel overwhelmed or stressed by them?\n\nPatient: Avoiding things? I mean, I still get the cleaning done at work, but I’m definitely a lot slower. I just second-guess my logs so much lately that I’ve actually stopped pushing for the supervisor track. Being a supervisor means carrying around that giant metal clipboard anyway. Have you seen those things? They weigh like five pounds and the spring clip always snaps down on your fingers if you aren't careful. My old boss, Dave, he actually lost a fingernail to one of those clips a few years ago. It just turned completely black and fell off in the middle of a shift while he was eating a turkey sandwich. Grossest thing I ever saw. He was eating it on this weird rye bread that had those little seeds in it—caraway seeds, I think? I hate those. They get stuck in your teeth for days. I had one stuck in my molar once right before a family get-together... which, actually, I've been skipping a lot of those lately. Family stuff, I mean. And those neighborhood clean-ups I used to make time for. I just... skip them. My aunt always makes this potato salad for the family things anyway, and she puts raisins in it. Who puts raisins in potato salad? It's a crime against mayonnaise, honestly. She says it adds sweet texture, but it just feels like you're chewing on a tick or something. \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Overwhelmed? Yeah, I guess I just avoid the extra stuff now, like the supervisor track or seeing family. I just second-guess myself too much to deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about the future or things that might happen?\n\nPatient: Worry about the future? I don't know, not really. I mean, I don't really think about the future much. It just seems like a lot of work to think that far ahead. Speaking of work, the floor buffer broke again yesterday. The cord got all tangled up around the wheels. It's one of those bright yellow cords, you know the ones? They’re supposed to be heavy-duty, but they always get those weird kinks in them if you don't wrap them up perfectly in a figure-eight pattern. My old roommate used to wrap his guitar cables like that. He was in this terrible garage band that practiced on Tuesdays. Why Tuesdays? I have no idea. But they played this awful cover of \"Free Bird\" that went on for like twenty minutes, and the bassist was always out of tune. He had this bass that was painted neon green with black splatters, like some kind of toxic waste barrel. He bought it at a pawn shop off the highway, the one next to that diner that serves breakfast 24/7. I went to that diner once and ordered pancakes, but they came out tasting like onions because they didn't clean the grill after making hash browns. Hash browns are only good if they're crispy anyway, otherwise it's just mushy potato strings. \n\nAnyway... what did you ask? Worrying? No, I don't really worry about what's going to happen. I just don't really care enough to worry, I guess. It's just one day after another.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don't know, maybe. I don't really do much anymore. I used to like building those little model airplanes on the weekends. The ones from World War II, mostly. I had this really cool P-51 Mustang kit that came with authentic decals and everything. The glue for those things is awful, though. It smells like nail polish remover and if you get it on your fingers, it dries into this weird crust that takes days to peel off. My cousin used to sniff that stuff when we were kids, which explains a lot about him now, honestly. He moved out to Arizona a few years ago to start an emu farm. Emus! Can you imagine? They're basically dinosaurs. I saw a video once of an emu chasing a guy on a bicycle and it was terrifying. They have those massive claws on their feet. I'd never want to live in Arizona anyway, it's way too hot. And you have to check your shoes for scorpions every morning. Imagine waking up, putting your foot in your sneaker, and getting stung by a scorpion. I only buy sneakers with velcro now anyway, because bending down to tie laces just feels like too much effort. \n\nWait, what was the question? Lost interest? Yeah, I don't really build the planes anymore. I just sit on the couch. Nothing really sounds fun.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight changes? Um, no, not really. My weight's been pretty much the same. I mean, my pants still fit exactly like they always do. I wear those dark blue work pants, you know, the ones with the reinforced knees? They're supposed to be stain-resistant, but bleach still ruins them. I spilled a whole bottle of bleach last month in the mop closet because the shelf bracket just gave out. The maintenance guy, Frank, he used drywall anchors instead of finding the stud when he put it up. Who does that for a shelf holding liquid chemicals? Frank’s always cutting corners like that. He drives this old beat-up Chevy truck that makes a terrible squealing noise every time he turns the steering wheel. Power steering fluid leak, probably. I had a car that did that once, a '98 Honda Civic. Great gas mileage, but the heater core blew in the middle of January. Have you ever driven on the highway with no heat in January? I had to wear my winter coat and a ski mask just to commute. I looked like I was about to rob a bank at 6 AM. The toll booth guy actually looked terrified when I handed him my money. \n\nAnyway... what did you ask? Weight? Yeah, no, my weight hasn't changed. Same as always.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? I don't know, I guess I just don't feel much of anything. It's like everything is just kind of gray, you know? Even things that used to be okay, like watching the game on Sunday. I used to watch football every week. My dad was a huge fan, he had this lucky jersey that he never washed. It smelled like old beer and stale cigarette smoke. He used to smoke those unfiltered cigarettes, the ones that come in the soft pack. I never understood how he could smoke those, they just seem so harsh. I tried one once when I was a teenager and coughed for like ten minutes straight. My throat felt like I swallowed sandpaper. You know what else feels like sandpaper? The cheap toilet paper they stock in the building where I work. It's that single-ply stuff that practically dissolves if you look at it wrong. The tenants complain about it all the time, but management won't spring for the good stuff. They're always trying to save a few pennies here and there. Like last week, they switched the soap dispensers from the foaming kind to this weird pink liquid that smells like bubblegum and hospital floors. It's so gross. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Pleasure? Yeah, I don't really get any happiness out of things. I just kind of exist, I guess. Nothing really matters that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel nervous or on edge most of the time?\n\nPatient: Nervous? On edge? No, not really. I don't get worked up about much. I'm too tired to be nervous, honestly. It takes energy to be on edge, you know? Like my neighbor's dog, this little terrier mix. That thing is always on edge. It barks at every single leaf that blows past the window. Yap, yap, yap, all day long. The owner, she's this older lady who always wears these bright floral muumuus, even in the middle of winter. She says the dog has anxiety and needs a special weighted blanket. A blanket for a dog! Can you believe that? I saw her carrying it into the elevator once. It looked like a miniature sleeping bag. Elevators are the worst place to get stuck with someone who wants to talk, especially when you're just trying to finish your shift and go home. The service elevator in my building is so slow, it takes a solid two minutes just to get from the basement to the lobby. And it makes this awful groaning sound halfway up, like the cables are about to snap. I told management about it, but they just told me to keep the floors buffed and stop worrying about the mechanics. I use this industrial buffer, it's heavy as hell. If you don't hold it just right, it'll swing you right into the wall. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Nervous? Oh, right. No, I don't feel nervous. I'm just... flat. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: Worrying about the future? Um, no, I don't really think about it. I mean, what's there to worry about? It's just gonna be the same as today, and yesterday. Just... more of the same. I don't really look forward to anything, so I don't worry about it either. It's like looking at a blank wall. Speaking of walls, the paint they use in the hallways at work is this awful beige color. They call it \"Navajo White,\" but it's not white at all. It's like the color of old oatmeal. And it shows every single scuff mark. People are always dragging their luggage or their bikes against it, and I'm the one who has to scrub it off with a Magic Eraser. Those things are amazing, by the way. I don't know what's in them, probably some kind of toxic chemical, but they take off everything. I tried using one on my sneakers once, these white New Balances I got on sale. It worked pretty well, but it kind of took the finish off the leather. Now they get dirty twice as fast. You ever try to keep white shoes clean in the winter? It's impossible. The salt on the sidewalks just eats right through the material. The city uses this cheap rock salt that's practically boulders. You step on it and it feels like you're walking on gravel. \n\nUh, sorry, what was the question? Worrying? Yeah, no. I don't worry about the future. I just don't really care what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Pleasure? Didn't you just ask me that? Or maybe that was someone else. I don't know, it all blurs together. But yeah, no pleasure. Like I said, everything's just gray. It's like watching a TV with the color turned all the way down. I used to have this old tube TV, you know, the big heavy ones that took two people to lift? I found it on the curb one trash day. People throw away perfectly good stuff all the time. At the building, I see it constantly. Last month, someone tossed out a whole set of golf clubs. Just left them by the dumpster. They were in this nice leather bag, too. I thought about taking them, maybe trying to learn how to play, but then I figured, when would I have the time? Or the energy? Plus, I don't even like being outside that much. The sun gives me a headache. And the golf courses around here are so expensive anyway. You have to pay just to walk on the grass. Grass is a pain to maintain, too. The landscaping crew at work is always complaining about the sprinkler system. They say the timer is busted and it goes off at 3 AM, flooding the flower beds. They planted these petunias that look like they're drowning half the time. \n\nAnyway, what was it? Pleasure? Yeah, no. I don't feel anything like that. Just... nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel fearful or apprehensive about everyday situations?\n\nPatient: Fearful? Like, scared of things? No, not really. I don't get scared of everyday stuff. It's just... I'm just here, you know? Like I'm just going through the motions. There's no fear, just a lot of dragging myself around. Speaking of dragging, the vacuum cleaner I use for the lobby carpets is an absolute nightmare. It's this huge, industrial thing with a cord that must be fifty feet long. I swear it weighs as much as a small car. The wheels on it are squeaky, too. Squeak, squeak, squeak, all the way down the hall. I tried putting WD-40 on them, but it didn't do a thing. I think the bearings are shot. You ever try to fix something and it just makes it worse? Like last week, the sink in my apartment was dripping. Just a slow drip, but it was driving me crazy at night. Drip... drip... drip. So, I got under there with a wrench, figured I could just tighten it up. Well, I tightened it alright, and the whole pipe cracked. Water everywhere. Had to call the super, and he was not happy about it. He's this guy named Frank, always wears these stained undershirts and smells like stale cigars. He spent an hour under my sink complaining about how they don't make pipes like they used to. He said it was all cheap PVC now, not like the good old copper pipes. Copper's expensive, though. People steal it out of abandoned buildings all the time. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Fearful? Yeah, no. I'm not scared of anything. I just don't have the energy for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel uneasy or have a sense of apprehension that persists even when there's no apparent reason for it?\n\nPatient: Uneasy? Um, no, not really. I don't get that feeling of apprehension or whatever. Like I said, it's just... flat. Everything's just flat. I don't get worked up over things that aren't there. I barely get worked up over the things that *are* there. Like the trash bins outside the building. They're these huge metal dumpsters, and the lids are always getting stuck. You have to heave them open, and half the time they slam back down on your hand. I've got this bruise on my knuckles right now from Tuesday. It's turning that weird yellowish-green color. It's funny how bruises do that, right? Like a mood ring, but for injuries. I had one on my shin once that looked exactly like a map of Florida. I got it from walking into the coffee table in the dark. My apartment has terrible lighting. The fixtures are all from the seventies, those ugly frosted glass domes that make everything look yellow. I tried changing the bulbs to those daylight LEDs, but then it just looked like a hospital room. Too bright, you know? It gave me a headache. I ended up just keeping the lights off most of the time anyway. It's cheaper on the electric bill, and it's not like I'm reading or doing anything that needs good light. \n\nSo, um, apprehension? No. I don't really feel that. Just... tired, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you've been feeling over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Changes? Over the past few months? I mean, not really changes, no. It's just... it's been the same for a long time. Most days I feel like there's this heavy, flat feeling following me around, you know? Even when I'm just pushing the cleaning cart down an empty hallway at work. The wheels on that cart, man, they're almost as bad as the vacuum cleaner. One of them is completely seized up, so you have to kind of shove it sideways to get it to turn a corner. It leaves these black scuff marks on the linoleum that I then have to go back and buff out. It's like I'm creating more work for myself just by doing my job. I asked the building manager for a new cart back in October, but he said it wasn't in the budget. Everything's always \"not in the budget.\" But they somehow had the budget to buy these giant, ugly modern art sculptures for the lobby. They look like twisted up paperclips. People are always leaving their coffee cups on the base of them, like they're tables. Then I have to clean up the coffee rings. \n\nAnd even when I'm not at work, like if I'm sitting with my family and trying to act normal, that heavy feeling is just... there. It doesn't go away. So, no, not really any changes. Just the same heavy flat thing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
